# PIPE-PAGES requirements

| ID | Requirement | Verification |
|---|---|---|
| `PIPE-PAGES-R001` | **Repository switcher kept in sync with the core repo list.** The documentation site's repository switcher is renamed and its repository list is completed to match the full declared set of core repositories, replacing any per-repository duplicate copies and staying synchronized with the shared parity check and release receipt. Meristem's now-obsolete generator for a standalone web UI deployment manifest is also removed, since the web UI runs within Arbora. Caller chain: The reusable workflow `.github/workflows/pages_pipeline.yml` runs `scripts/sync_mkdocs_theme.py` `main` (line 153), which calls `sync_theme` (line 112) to check the consumer site against `templates/mkdocs-theme/`; `scripts/check_five_repo_parity.py` `main` (line 45) calls `verify` in `scripts/pages_fleet_parity.py:60` for each declared consumer. Shape: The one switcher list is `extra.ecosystem.sites` in `templates/mkdocs-theme/base.mkdocs.yml`, equal to `REPOS` in `scripts/skill_graph/model.py:15`; the declared consumer set is read by `load` in `scripts/pages_fleet_declaration.py:94`; reuse `ThemeFile` in `scripts/sync_mkdocs_theme.py:14` and add no second list. | Run `tests/test_spec_delivery_nav.py::test_site_urls_match_repos`. A parity check confirms the switcher's repository list matches the declared core-repository set and release receipt, and a source scan confirms the obsolete standalone deployment generator no longer exists. |
| `PIPE-PAGES-R002` | **Layered documentation from overview to reference.** The published documentation presents a layered information architecture: concise top-level introductions, then per-component overviews, then detailed reference pages, so a reader can go only as deep as they need. Caller chain: `scripts/build_skill_graph.py` calls `main` in `scripts/skill_graph/cli.py:149`, which runs `_run_pages_mode` (line 118) and `_run_components_mode` (line 106); the renderers in `scripts/skill_graph/render_pages.py` and `scripts/skill_graph/render_components.py` write the pages. Shape: Three tiers from one corpus: `render_index_md` (`scripts/skill_graph/render_pages.py:61`) for introductions, `render_components_eg` (`scripts/skill_graph/render_components.py:91`) for component pages and `render_concepts_md` (line 16) for reference; navigation from `extract_nav` in `scripts/skill_graph/nav.py:75`. | Run `tests/test_skill_graph_registry_comparison.py::test_all_public_skill_graph_tiers_project_one_corpus`. A navigation-depth and link-structure audit confirms each tier, introduction, component, and reference, exists and links progressively into the next. |
| `PIPE-PAGES-R003` | **Present-tense docs with accessible embedded start.** The published README and documentation home present content in the intended canonical order, include an embedded observable quick-start demonstration, use accessible multimodal and shared visual assets, run on the shared Material-themed site with short navigation, and describe the running engine's current behavior in present tense rather than its history. Caller chain: MkDocs builds each consumer site in `.github/workflows/pages_pipeline.yml`; `scripts/sync_mkdocs_theme.py` `main` (line 153) installs the shared theme and `pipelines-hook public-surface` checks the README order through `pipelines_hooks/docs/public_surface.py`. Shape: The theme is `templates/mkdocs-theme/base.mkdocs.yml` plus `templates/mkdocs-theme/overrides/main.html`; the README and quick-start checks live in `pipelines_hooks/docs/public_surface.py` and `pipelines_hooks/docs/public_surface_quickstart.py`; reuse both and add no second checker. | Run `tests/test_sync_mkdocs_theme.py::test_theme_components_use_accessible_native_controls`. Strict documentation build, theme, accessibility, and content-diff checks confirm the README order, embedded start, shared visuals, and present-tense wording. |
| `PIPE-PAGES-R004` | **One progressively disclosed skill-graph corpus.** Concepts, components, and reference pages are drawn from one progressively disclosed skill-graph content corpus rather than separate, independently maintained sources. Caller chain: `scripts/build_skill_graph.py` calls `main` in `scripts/skill_graph/cli.py:149`, which runs `_run_pages_mode` (line 118) and `_run_components_mode` (line 106); the renderers in `scripts/skill_graph/render_pages.py` and `scripts/skill_graph/render_components.py` write the pages. Shape: `scripts/skill_graph/corpus.py` builds the one corpus dict; every renderer in `scripts/skill_graph/render_pages.py` and `scripts/skill_graph/render_components.py` takes that dict as input; reuse it and add no second source. | Run `tests/test_skill_graph_registry_comparison.py::test_all_public_skill_graph_tiers_project_one_corpus`. A build check confirms the published concept, component, and reference pages are generated from the single skill-graph corpus with consistent navigation depth. |
| `PIPE-PAGES-R005` | **Generate architecture and reference pages from registries.** Architecture, capability, and reference documentation pages are generated directly from the underlying registries and contracts rather than hand-authored, so published content stays consistent with the implementation it describes. Caller chain: `scripts/build_skill_graph.py` calls `main` in `scripts/skill_graph/cli.py:149`, which runs `_run_pages_mode` (line 118) and `_run_components_mode` (line 106); the renderers in `scripts/skill_graph/render_pages.py` and `scripts/skill_graph/render_components.py` write the pages. Shape: `render_components_eg` (`scripts/skill_graph/render_components.py:91`) renders from the registry; `_write_or_check` (`scripts/skill_graph/cli.py:22`) compares the committed pages with the render in check mode; reuse both. | Run `tests/test_skill_graph_registry_comparison.py::test_component_registry_page_matches_its_registry`. A build-time comparison confirms each generated page's content matches its source registry or contract. |
| `PIPE-PAGES-R006` | **Rehome generated docs out of the legacy docs directory.** Every generated or gate-checked artifact currently stored under a repository's legacy docs directory is moved to its new canonical tracked location, every quality gate that referenced the old location is repointed to the new one, and the legacy docs directory is then removed. Caller chain: `pipelines-hook root-hygiene` runs `main` at `pipelines_hooks/cli.py:151` and `run_gate` at `pipelines_hooks/cli.py:101`; it reads the allowed root directories from `.config/repo-layout.toml`. Shape: Artifacts live under `reference/` (`reference/pages-readiness.md`, `reference/publication-identity.md`, `reference/python-release-readiness.md`); `.config/repo-layout.toml` lists `reference` and no `docs`; reuse `pipelines_hooks/hygiene/root.py`. | Run `tests/test_docs_rehomed.py::test_the_legacy_docs_directory_no_longer_exists`. A source scan confirms no gate or build step still reads from the removed docs directory and that each migrated artifact exists at its new tracked location. |
| `PIPE-PAGES-R007` | **Ecosystem flow diagram with exact names and project marks.** The documentation home page renders the shared Material theme chrome together with an architecture flow diagram showing the path from people through the Arbora web UI, Tuig, Axil, and messaging/protocol clients to Arbora, Meristem, and Medulla, plus the source-system-to-Scion SDK flow, using each project's exact display title and a versioned logo mark. Caller chain: The reusable workflow `.github/workflows/pages_pipeline.yml` runs `scripts/sync_mkdocs_theme.py` `main` (line 153), which calls `sync_theme` (line 112) to check the consumer site against `templates/mkdocs-theme/`; `scripts/check_five_repo_parity.py` `main` (line 45) calls `verify` in `scripts/pages_fleet_parity.py:60` for each declared consumer. Shape: The diagram markup is theme-owned in `templates/mkdocs-theme/overrides/main.html`; the project marks are the seven assets in `templates/mkdocs-theme/assets/`; display names come from `REPOS` in `scripts/skill_graph/model.py:15`; `_runtime_reference_mismatches` (`scripts/sync_mkdocs_theme.py:92`) rejects a local diagram copy. | Run `tests/test_sync_mkdocs_theme.py::test_all_seven_product_logo_assets_remain_canonical`. Focused theme rendering tests plus a changed-file documentation build confirm the diagram's flow, project titles, and logo marks render correctly. |
| `PIPE-PAGES-R008` | **Correct ownership and quick start in consumer parity checks.** The shared Pages parity check verifies that a consumer site's documentation correctly states process and front-door ownership, includes a stdio-based quick start, renders on the shared Material theme with short navigation, and keeps its content flows accessible. A multi-page render, link, and accessibility audit plus a changed-file build check confirm ownership statements, the quick start, and accessible navigation on the consumer site. |

| `PIPE-PAGES-R042` | **One generated architecture explorer, always current.** The canonical architecture explorer is published at `https://knuckles-team.github.io/.github/architecture/`. The organization site builds it with this generator in github mode every 30 minutes and on a `specs-changed` repository dispatch; the reusable `notify_org_site.yml` is the one implementation that sends that dispatch, and each core repository calls it from `.github/workflows/org-site.yml` on every push to main with no path filter. It does nothing, and exits 0, until both optional `org_site_app_id` and `org_site_app_private_key` secrets exist (the org secrets `ORG_SITE_APP_ID` and `ORG_SITE_APP_PRIVATE_KEY` reach it through `secrets: inherit`), then mints a GitHub App token for `.github` with a SHA-pinned `actions/create-github-app-token` and posts with it; a failed mint or post only warns. Github mode scans every public, unarchived organization repository that has `specs/`, so a new repository appears and a removed repository, row or connector part disappears on the next build. The explorer places each row on the deepest architecture part, lists each row that it cannot place under an Unmapped part and resolves each row's spec family through its repository's spec aliases before matching `model.json`, so a prefix rename never unmaps a row, and fails the build until a part owns it, with an error annotation that groups the unmapped rows by `repo:FAMILY` and names the fix (the reviewed allowlist stays empty, so a new row needs a part in `model.json`, for example OVERSTORY-APPS-R050 on the composed deployment acceptance part), and stamps its corner with the build time and the head commit of every scanned repository. A pinned layered layout engine precomputes every level, so the page needs no runtime layout. Each core repository site serves `/architecture/` as a redirect to the canonical explorer that keeps the `#<node>` anchor, and the shared theme links it from every page at the node of the current repository. A content page that shows the static runtime diagram must also link the explorer. | `tests/test_architecture_explorer.py` places fixture rows, proves a deleted row disappears, reconciles discovery, fits the connector fleet, checks the stamp and builds the page and redirect variants offline; `tests/test_architecture_canonical.py` proves a family renamed through a spec alias still maps, an exact row alias never renames a spec directory, a refused build names its snapshot errors and its unmapped `repo:FAMILY` groups with the fix, and `model.json` uses no prefix retired in any repository's alias map; `tests/test_pages_pipeline_contract.py` and `tests/test_spec_delivery_nav.py` check the workflow step, the dispatch and the theme link; `tests/test_sync_mkdocs_theme.py::test_check_requires_the_diagram_to_link_the_explorer` checks the diagram link. |

Repeated strings are stored once in shared tables, so the index adds no row text beyond the title; search also reads each row's description from the page's own rows (R042.6). | `PIPE-PAGES-R048` | **Each repository box has a code view built from the Medulla code graph.** Caller: the Canopy build reads one code-graph snapshot per repository from Medulla. The build never parses source. The snapshot holds code items, their edges and a content digest per item. Communities come from Leiden clustering with a fixed seed, so equal input gives equal output. A rebuild re-clusters only the items whose digest changed and their direct neighbours. All other items keep their community. The knob `canopy.code_view.full_recluster_share` triggers a full re-cluster when the changed share exceeds it. The starting value is 0.2. Each spec row links to the code items that its test bindings and named files reach. Each code item links back to those rows. A repository without a snapshot shows no code view and no error. Depends on: MEDULLA-SDLC-R012. Caller chain: `scripts/architecture_explorer/build.py` `main` (line 447) calls `build_with_sources` (line 302) and `render` (line 398), which write the page; `scripts/architecture_explorer/template.html` runs in the browser. | `PIPE-PAGES-R049` | **Canopy uses one frosted-glass material on every surface.** The material has three levels as tokens: chrome, control and reading. Every surface uses one of them. This covers the header, toolbar, panel, search results, expanded rows, details blocks, map overlays and the progress panel. Each level has a backdrop blur, a hairline edge and a 1 px top highlight. A surface layered on another glass surface stays translucent, and its fill alpha never exceeds its level's alpha. Buttons, zoom controls, toolbar toggles, chips, badges, status labels, breadcrumbs, the legend and the search input must use the control level. Pressed and selected states must stay translucent with a higher-alpha glass tint. Their text must keep a contrast of at least 4.5:1. Canvas-drawn nodes must not use backdrop blur. Opaque fills appear only under reduced transparency or forced colors. Caller chain: The CSS tokens in `template.html` style every surface. Shape: Three glass levels as CSS custom properties; each surface class uses one. | `tests/test_architecture_explorer.py::test_every_surface_uses_a_glass_level_and_layers_stay_translucent`. |
| `PIPE-PAGES-R050` | **Navigation motion is satisfying, and the background moves with it.** A pressed control scales to 0.97 and springs back within 180 ms. Choosing a part flies the camera, and the chosen node pulses once on arrival. The background scene reads the camera on the same animation frame. Its waves shift with camera pan by a parallax factor, and its tint eases toward the focused part's layer colour. The background starts and ends each flight with the camera. Between flights the background returns to its slow drift. Motion uses transforms and opacity only, never layout. Reduced motion removes the parallax, the scale and the pulse, and the tint changes at once. The ocean motion control stops all background motion. Caller chain: `scripts/architecture_explorer/build.py` `main` (line 447) calls `build_with_sources` (line 302) and `render` (line 398), which write the page; `scripts/architecture_explorer/template.html` runs in the browser.
